P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

F1052nd of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: price is the whole story and it is a negative contribution after Q1 2027. Var has no pricing power; its realised price is benchmark plus a small, market-dependent differential, minus contractual lags in rising markets and plus them in falling ones. On my deck the realised price falls from about USD 100/boe (FY2026) to 87 (FY2027) and 71 (FY2028), which removes about USD 4.4bn of revenue between FY2026 and FY2028 against about USD 1.0bn added by volume (mostly BlueNord), and takes about 5 points off the EBITDA margin. The structure of Var's realisation turns from a drag to a cushion as prices fall, which slightly flattens the decline in H1 2027, and BlueNord's mandatory hedges dilute the group's price exposure in 2027.

Revenue bridge, FY2026 to FY2028 (derived). Volume: +39 kboepd (386 to 425, mostly BlueNord's 43 kboepd) at USD 71/boe, about +USD 1.0bn. Price: USD 99.9 to 71.3 per boe on about 155 mmboe, about -USD 4.4bn. Net -USD 3.0bn (-21%). Beyond FY2028 price is broadly flat on the deck and volume slightly declining, so revenue drifts down about 3% a year.

How the realisation structure behaves along the deck.

  • Crude (about 65% of volume; 58% at BlueNord). Var realised USD 6/bbl above dated Brent in Q2 and Q3 2026 and guided USD 6-10 for Q4 (some cargoes up to USD 20, Norne). These premiums are a product of physical tightness, not of Var: on 9 November 2026 Morgan Stanley reported dated Brent below front-month futures with 36 North Sea cargoes unsold, and Equinor cut its Oseberg premium over dated from USD 8.40 to USD 1.10/bbl. Because cargoes are priced weeks ahead, Q4 2026 still captures September-October premiums; I use USD +7 in Q4 2026, USD +2-3 in 2027 and USD +1 from 2028. Aker BP's 6% higher realisation in Q1 2026 (liquids USD 82.20 against Var's 78.30) came from pricing against physical dated Brent, crude quality (Johan Sverdrup) and timing; none is something Var can replicate quickly.
  • Gas (about 33% of standalone volume; 42% at BlueNord). Q3 2026: realised USD 108/boe, about USD 18 below the spot reference, because 29% of sales were fixed or year-ahead at about USD 87 and 25% month-ahead lagged a rising market by about USD 22. About 30% of Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 gas is fixed at about USD 82/boe, so Q4 2026 realisation (I use about USD 128/boe at TTF EUR 85) lags spot by about USD 40. From Q1 2027, on a falling deck, the month-ahead lag flips to a premium (I use +USD 4-5/boe in H1 2027), which the company itself says ("the impact will be the opposite in a falling market"). Equinor (70% day-ahead, fully unhedged) captures spot faster both ways; DNO's Engie contract (implied USD 5/mmbtu) is the opposite extreme.
  • Marketing premium. Norne's claim of about USD 1.3bn above spot over two-year cycles (2 April 2025) is unsubstantiated by disclosure; in the base case I give it no explicit value beyond the crude differential.
  • BlueNord's hedges. BlueNord's reserve-based loan required hedging 50% of oil in year one and 40% in year two, and 50% of gas in seasons one and two falling to 20% by season four; Q3 2026 hedges were at USD 65.40/bbl oil and EUR 31/MWh gas. On my deck these cost about USD 0.2bn of revenue in 2027 against spot (derived), falling to near zero by 2029. Under acquisition accounting these out-of-the-money hedges may be recognised as a liability at fair value at completion, in which case reported revenue and EBITDA in 2027 would be about USD 0.2bn higher than my base with the cash unchanged; the Consumer should check the first consolidated quarter (Q1 2027 results).
  • Var's own hedges are small (collars on 13% of remaining 2026 post-tax oil near USD 75/bbl; 15% of Q4 2026 post-tax gas with a USD 83 floor and USD 224 cap) and not binding on my deck.

Contribution to margin. With a fixed USD 2.2bn cash cost, each USD 10/boe of price moves the EBITDA margin by about 1.5-2 points at USD 70-90/boe. The base path takes the margin from 86.2% (FY2026) to 84.1% (FY2027) and 80.9% (FY2028); margin then holds about 80-81% because the deck is flat. At a FY2025-like realised price (USD 66/boe) the FY2028 margin would be about 79%, close to the FY2025 81.2%, despite BlueNord's higher costs.

Quarterly shape for the next 18 months (Part 3 of the forecast inputs). Revenue growth against the same quarter a year earlier: +89.5% (Q4 2026), +51.2% (Q1 2027), -7.9% (Q2 2027), -13.6% (Q3 2027), -24.8% (Q4 2027), -25.9% (Q1 2028). EBITDA margin: 88.3%, 86.4%, 84.2%, 82.8%, 82.3%, 82.0%. The year-on-year comparison turns negative in Q2 2027 and is deepest in Q4 2027 and Q1 2028, when the comparison is against the winter-gas quarters and before the Balder FPU saving.

What would change the call. TTF staying above EUR 60/MWh through the 2027 refill season (the memo's own revisit trigger), or the FY2028 consensus EPS line continuing to rise with the nearer years (+11% in the last month), would say the price deck is too low. A Hormuz reopening or a mild winter (Morgan Stanley bear TTF EUR 55 this winter) would bring the H1 2027 normalisation forward and remove most of my FY2027 premium to consensus.

Where I differ from the first set. The first set inferred that consensus FY2027 revenue of USD 12.9bn implies about USD 78-82/boe. I hold FY2027 at USD 87/boe because Q1 2027 carries winter gas at a time when storage is at a 15-year low for the season and Russian LNG is banned from January 2027; my difference to consensus is concentrated in Q1 2027 (about USD 0.5bn of revenue) and is the most fragile part of my FY2027 number.

5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7525P-0112
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

F2550th of 54

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: very high leverage to price, moderate to volume, and low to cost; because price changes fall almost entirely to EBIT, a 10% price move changes EBIT by about 16% at 2026 levels and about 21% at the 2028 deck, and the range of 2027 EPS outcomes spans roughly USD 0.35 to 0.85.

  • Cost structure: production cost about USD 1.6bn a year (about USD 11/boe, largely fixed and NOK-denominated: about 70% local salaries); exploration expense about USD 0.25-0.3bn; depreciation about USD 3.3-3.5bn (unit-of-production, so it moves with volume, not price); no royalties.
  • Price leverage (derived): a 10% revenue move from price is about USD 1.4bn in 2026 against EBIT of USD 8.7bn (16-17%), and about USD 1.1bn in 2028 against EBIT of USD 5.0bn (21-22%). Net income moves by the same percentage because tax is proportional.
  • Volume leverage (derived): an extra barrel earns price less variable cost and depreciation, about USD 50-55/boe at an USD 80/boe deck, so 20 kboepd is worth about USD 0.4bn of EBIT, about USD 0.09bn of net income and about USD 0.03 of EPS.
  • Cost leverage (derived): USD 1/boe of unit cost is about USD 0.16bn of EBIT and about USD 0.013 of EPS.
  • Range: the 2027 broker EPS spread (USD 0.47 to 0.915) mirrors price decks of roughly USD 70 to 105/boe; adding volume and tax noise, the Consumer should carry a cone of about USD 0.35-0.85 for 2027 EPS, against a 0.596 consensus.
P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

F1252nd of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the operating leverage is now higher than at any point in the last six years and it is pointed at a falling price, so it converts a 29% fall in realised price from FY2026 to FY2028 (about USD 100 to 71 per boe; revenue falls 21% because BlueNord adds 10% to volume) into a 38% fall in EBIT and a 58% fall in EPS. The cause is structural: the 2023-2026 build-out replaced a variable-feeling cost base with a large fixed one (cash cost about USD 2.1-2.3bn a year, 70% in NOK, plus D&A of about USD 3.6-3.75bn that is fixed per barrel at about USD 24/boe). That leverage works in both directions, but within the forecast horizon the price path, not the leverage, decides which direction.

How large the fixed base is, and what it does (derived from the base case).

  • Cash operating cost: about USD 14/boe all-in in FY2027 (standalone production cost USD 11.2/boe, BlueNord about USD 19/boe all-in during its Tyra ramp, plus about USD 300m a year of other operating cost and overhead), roughly USD 2.2bn a year. About 70% is NOK-denominated salaries and services, so it does not fall with the oil price and rises with krone strength.
  • D&A: about USD 24/boe, against about USD 13-14/boe in FY2022-FY2023 (USD 1.56-1.58bn on 202-213 kboepd). The overruns at Balder X (USD 4.3bn, doubled) and Johan Castberg now sit in the depreciation base, and BlueNord's assets will come in at fair value (BlueNord's own unit depreciation USD 14-16.2/boe; I use about USD 19/boe after purchase-price accounting, because Var paid about 1.24-1.3x BlueNord's NAV).
  • Together, about USD 38/boe of cost sits ahead of EBIT. That is the EBIT breakeven realised price, against USD 87/boe in FY2027 and USD 71/boe in FY2028. At a USD 70/boe deck, 54% of revenue is consumed by cost before tax; at USD 100/boe, 38%.

The degree of operating leverage rises as the price falls. A 10% move in revenue from price moves EBIT by about 16% at FY2026 levels (revenue/EBIT = 1.65), about 18% in FY2027 and about 21% in FY2028 (2.1x). Net income moves by the same percentage as EBIT because tax is proportional; EPS moves by more in FY2027 (10% dilution and fixed hybrid coupons). In money: each USD 10/boe of realised price is about USD 1.55bn of revenue and EBITDA a year at 425-428 kboepd, about USD 0.32bn of net income and USD 0.116-0.118 of EPS on 2,744.8m shares. Volume leverage is much lower: 10 kboepd is about USD 160m of EBIT at a USD 71/boe deck (price less about USD 3/boe of variable cost and USD 24/boe of unit-of-production D&A), USD 35m of net income and USD 0.013 of EPS. Cost leverage is lowest: USD 1/boe of unit cost is about USD 155m of EBITDA and USD 0.012 of EPS.

Against the last four to six years.

FY2021 FY2022 FY2023 FY2024 FY2025 FY2027 (base) FY2028 (base)
EBITDA margin (FactSet operating) 78.9% 86.7% 81.2% 78.2% 81.2% 84.1% 80.9%
EBIT margin 48.7% 70.7% 58.0% 48.8% 42.9% 56.6% 47.9%
D&A, USDm 1,821 1,564 1,580 2,170 3,046 3,750 3,650
  • The EBITDA margin is roughly where it has always been for a given price (high because there are no royalties and the cash cost per barrel is moderate). What has changed is the EBIT margin at a given price: FY2022's 70.7% EBIT margin came on a high price and USD 1.56bn of D&A FY2028 at a similar EBITDA margin (80.9%) gives only about 48% because D&A is 2.3 times larger. The first set's point that "depreciation per barrel has risen structurally" is confirmed and, if anything, understated: consensus D&A (USD 3.49bn in FY2027, 3.32bn in FY2028) looks USD 0.26-0.33bn light against BlueNord's addition, which overstates consensus EBIT by about 4-6% (I agree with the first set here).
  • Historical evidence that the leverage works as described: FY2023 revenue -30% gave EBIT -43% (a leverage of 1.4 on a smaller fixed base); FY2022 revenue +62% gave EBIT +135%. The fixed base is now larger, so the same price swing produces bigger percentage moves.

What the leverage does to each main line over the horizon (base case).

  • Revenue: the price, not volume, sets the path (P-04): USD 14.1bn (FY2026) to 13.6bn (FY2027) to 11.1bn (FY2028), then flat to slightly down.
  • EBITDA margin: compresses about 5 points from FY2026 to FY2028 (86.2% to 80.9%), because the USD 2.2bn cash cost does not fall with price; it then drifts down about 1 point to FY2030 as carbon tax and wage inflation (4.4-4.6% Norwegian settlements) offset the Balder FPU saving.
  • EBIT: falls faster than EBITDA (USD 8.5bn to 5.3bn, -38%) because D&A rises with BlueNord while revenue falls.
  • EPS: falls fastest (USD 0.798 to 0.339, -58%) because the share count rises 10% and hybrid coupons and finance costs are fixed.

The krone is the one fixed-cost exposure the company does not hedge, and its largest effect is not on cost. A 10% stronger krone raises USD opex by about USD 150m (70% of USD 2.2bn) and NOK capex by about USD 160m (60% of about USD 2.65bn development), together about USD 0.07bn after tax a year. Far larger, but non-cash and one-off: under the USD functional currency, tax payable is held in NOK, so the same move revalues a tax payable of roughly USD 3bn by about USD 0.3bn of tax expense in the quarter of the move (about USD 0.10-0.11 of EPS). Q2 2026 (FX gain on tax balances USD 160.3m, 65% effective tax rate) and Q3 2026 (loss USD 71.8m, 85%) show the mechanism at work. Nordea forecasts USD/NOK at 9.82 in mid-2027 and 9.19 by end-2027, i.e. krone strength while oil normalises, which is the combination where operating leverage and tax translation hurt together. Aker BP hedges 50-70% of planned NOK spend through 2027; Var hedges none.

Skew and cone. Leverage widens the cone symmetrically, but the base price path is falling, so for the 18-month horizon the leverage mainly amplifies the downside. FY2027 EPS spans about USD 0.27 (Brent 65, TTF 35 from Q1 2027, 10 kboepd lower volume) to USD 0.82 (Brent 95, TTF 75 held through 2027), around a base of 0.513.

Financial leverage

C-7660P-0235

P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

F3551st of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: financial leverage is comfortable for the balance sheet and material for EPS. Net interest-bearing debt stays below about 0.5x EBITDA (below about 0.75x with hybrids as debt) through FY2030 on my base deck, far inside the 1.3x ceiling; but finance costs and hybrid coupons together take about USD 0.077 a share from FY2027 EPS (15%) and about USD 0.08 from FY2028 EPS (23%), which is more than every cost-efficiency lever combined (P-03). The real balance-sheet risk is not the level of debt but its direction in a fast price fall, when the tax lag and a defended dividend push debt up at the same time; in my bear case hybrid-adjusted leverage reaches about 1.1x by end-2028, which is where a dividend cut becomes likely.

The finance-cost line is stepping up, not down.

  • FY2023-FY2024 net interest expense was only USD 21-23m (FactSet), because USD 358m (FY2024) and USD 220m (FY2025) of interest was capitalised on Balder X and Johan Castberg at 6.45-7.21%. That tailwind has ended with start-up.
  • Q3 2026 net financial expense was USD 83.5m before FX (USD 334m annualised), already including about USD 3.9bn of asset-retirement obligation accretion (non-cash, roughly USD 140m a year on Var standalone).
  • FY2027 adds: a full year of the USD 2.5bn senior notes at about 5.3% (USD 500m repaid, so about USD 2.0bn net new debt, about USD 106m a year, partly offset by interest income while the cash is held); BlueNord's debt (reserve-based loan USD 725m drawn, BNOR18 USD 400m at 7.875%, BNOR17 hybrid USD 300m at 12%, stepping to 17% in January 2030); accretion on BlueNord's USD 1.37bn of abandonment obligations (about USD 50m a year); and lower interest income as cash is spent on the H1 2027 tax catch-up. I set net financial items at USD -480m in FY2027, easing to USD -440m by FY2030 as BlueNord's debt is refinanced (the financing half of the USD 250-300m post-tax synergies) and offset by slowly rising net debt. Cash interest is about USD 250-290m a year; the rest is non-cash accretion.
  • After tax, at the 79% rate I apply, this is about USD 100m a year, USD 0.037 a share. The deductibility of finance costs against the 71.8% special tax is partial, which is one reason I use 79% not 78% in FY2027.

Hybrid coupons are the larger per-share drag and are routinely mis-modelled. EUR 750m at 7.862% (first call: 15 November 2028 per the 2025 annual report as cited by AlphaSense; the first set gave February 2029, so the Consumer should treat the date as late 2028 to early 2029) and EUR 750m at 4.95% (first call 29 July 2031): about USD 110m a year, deducted from net income before EPS (Q2 2026 adjustment corrected from USD 30.4m to 24.5m; my FY2026 total about USD 97m because the 4.95% issue came in April 2026). That is USD 0.040 a share, about 8% of FY2027 EPS and 12% of FY2028 EPS. Refinancing the 7.862% tranche at about 5-5.5% at the first call saves about USD 20m a year (USD 0.007 a share): trivial for EPS, so the call date matters for credit, not earnings. Not calling would trigger a coupon reset against five-year EUR mid-swaps plus margin and step-up.

The balance sheet path (base case).

  • End-2026: NIBD about USD 2.3bn (Q3 USD 3.11bn; Q4 free cash flow about USD 1.2bn after USD 1.7bn of cash tax, less the USD 400m Q3 dividend and coupons), 0.19x.
  • End-2027: about USD 3.2bn after consolidating BlueNord (net debt about USD 1.3bn including its hybrid, plus USD 204m cash consideration, less about USD 0.3bn of Goliat and Fenja disposal proceeds), 0.28x; 0.43x with Var's hybrids as debt.
  • End-2028: about USD 4.1bn, 0.45x (0.65x with hybrids): the year free cash flow falls short of dividends and coupons by about USD 0.85bn (P-08).
  • FY2029-FY2030: roughly flat at about USD 4.2-4.3bn, 0.48-0.52x (0.68-0.72x), because free cash flow roughly equals dividends plus coupons at a USD 72-73 Brent and EUR 30-32 TTF deck once the tax lag has cleared.
  • Liquidity is not a constraint at any point: USD 7.5bn at Q3 2026 (USD 4.75bn cash, USD 2.75bn undrawn). Headroom to 1.3x is about USD 7-10bn of extra debt.

The equity side is the weak point. Book equity was about USD 1.9bn at Q3 2026, of which USD 1.75bn is hybrid capital, so ordinary equity is close to nil. BlueNord adds about USD 1.3bn of equity (248.4m shares at about USD 5.20). From FY2028 net income (about USD 1.0bn) less hybrid coupons (USD 0.11bn) is below dividends (USD 1.4bn), so ordinary equity shrinks by about USD 0.45-0.5bn a year (derived). Return on equity is therefore meaningless for this company; the Consumer should use return on capital employed and free cash flow per share. Morgan Stanley and Jefferies adjust leverage for hybrids (0.4x reported against 0.7x adjusted at Q2 2026); a rating agency that gave less equity credit would move the conversation from "0.3x" to "0.7x" without any change in the business.

Bear case (Brent USD 65 in FY2027 and 60 in FY2028, TTF EUR 35 and 28, 10 kboepd lower volume; derived). Free cash flow about USD 0 in FY2027 (the H1 2027 catch-up of about USD 3.5bn on 2026 income is still paid in full) and about USD 0.5-0.75bn in FY2028; after a USD 350m-a-quarter dividend, NIBD rises to about USD 6bn by end-2028, about 0.9x EBITDA and 1.1x with hybrids. That is the point at which I expect the board to fall back to the 25-30% of cash flow policy (about USD 0.95-1.15bn a year), i.e. a cut of 20-30%. The first set placed the dividend's invalidate level at Brent below USD 60; I agree, and add that the speed of the fall matters as much as the level, because the tax lag makes the first year of a fall far more expensive in cash than the second.

Where I differ from the first set. The first set called the USD 350m quarterly dividend a floor "management will defend with debt". On my base deck the debt needed is modest (about USD 1.9bn cumulative from FY2027 to FY2030, half of it BlueNord's own debt) and leverage never leaves the 0.3-0.5x band, so the defence is cheap; the dividend is threatened by the bear deck, not the base deck.

Cost-out scope NTM

C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

F2551st of 54

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: little that matters after tax: cost and capex cuts deliver about 22-28 cents to equity per dollar because the state shares 78% of every saving, and the one large discretionary cash item that flows one-for-one to cash is the dividend.

  • Production opex (about USD 1.6bn): partly reducible; the Balder FPU retirement in 2028 (about USD 130m a year gross), returning to the old USD 10/boe target (about USD 0.16bn pre-tax), and BlueNord overhead synergies. About 70% is Norwegian salaries, which are sticky; the FX-driven part reverses only if the krone weakens. Realistic: USD 0.5-1.0/boe over two to three years, worth about USD 0.02-0.04bn of net income a year (derived).
  • Exploration (USD 250-300m): fully discretionary, but 78% is refunded, so the post-tax saving is about USD 55-65m.
  • Development capex (USD 2.5-2.7bn; about 70% of long-term capex uncommitted): cuttable, but each dollar cut costs volume against an 8-9% decline and saves only about 22-28 cents after tax (DNB Carnegie: "negligible cash flow benefit from capex cuts").
  • Abandonment (about USD 200m a year; asset-retirement obligation USD 3.889bn): regulated, not cuttable; at risk of upward revision (USD 242.3m increase from estimate changes in Q3 2026).
  • Overheads and finance: BlueNord's synergies (USD 250-300m post-tax over 2027-2032) are mostly financing and overhead.
  • Dividend (USD 1.4-1.6bn a year): the only large lever that improves free cash flow after distributions one-for-one; management's behaviour says it will be the last lever pulled.
P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

F2551st of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: very little reaches EPS, and late. The identifiable gross savings are about USD 0.25-0.30bn a year pre-tax by FY2029 (Balder FPU retirement, BlueNord financing and overhead synergies, a partial return of unit cost towards USD 10.6-10.9/boe, a lower exploration budget), but after the 78% tax take, the krone, wage inflation and a rising carbon tax, the net amount that reaches net income is about USD 0.05-0.08bn a year by FY2029 (USD 0.02-0.03 a share), roughly what a USD 2-3/boe move in realised price does. Nothing material reaches the FY2027 line items; the first visible step is H2 2028.

The levers, sized and dated (pre-tax unless stated; derived from the AlphaSense report and the first set).

Why so little reaches net income. Every dollar of opex saved is worth 22 cents after tax; every dollar of capex saved on pre-2023 projects with the 86.9% deduction is worth about 13 cents in the first year (DNB Carnegie, 16 September 2026). The state co-owns 78% of every saving. The levers that bypass the tax wedge are the financing synergies (interest is not fully deductible against the special tax, so its saving is worth more after tax) and the dividend itself.

Track record of delivery (the reason to lean, not to assume). Positive: Neptune synergies rose from USD 300m to 500m (March 2024) to 600m, with over 50% inside nine months; the quarterly cost reached USD 10/boe in Q4 2025 on high uptime after nine start-ups. Negative: that USD 10/boe was not repeated (USD 10.4 Q1 2026, 11.2 Q2, 11.1 Q3); 2026 guidance was raised from about USD 10 to 11, with only USD 0.5/boe explained by currency; operated production efficiency fell from 97% (Q1 2026) to 91% (nine months). Unit cost is primarily a function of uptime and volume, not of cost programmes: at a fixed cash cost of about USD 1.55bn standalone, a 4-point recovery in efficiency (about 15 kboepd) lowers unit cost by about USD 0.45/boe without any cost being removed. So the most valuable "efficiency" lever is uptime, and it is the one the record is weakest on.

Peer precedents. Aker BP guides USD 8.5/boe for 2026 (raised from 8.0 on turnarounds) and hedges NOK; Equinor targets 50% lower tie-back cost and lead time and 30% lower brownfield modification cost (unverified); DNO cut North Sea staff after Sval; Harbour's unit opex rose from USD 12.8 to 13.9/boe as it integrated diverse assets. The pattern on the shelf is that cost leadership comes from operating model and uptime (Aker BP's alliances), not from programmes, and that integrations raise group unit cost before they lower it. BlueNord fits that pattern: its all-in unit cost (USD 23/boe in Q3 2026; USD 13 direct field opex at full Tyra capacity) is above Var's, so the combination raises group unit cash cost by about USD 0.6-0.8/boe in FY2027 before synergies.

When, in summary. FY2027: net zero to slightly negative (BlueNord's higher cost, krone, partial financing synergies). FY2028: about +USD 0.01 a share (half-year Balder FPU, financing synergies). FY2029-FY2030: about +USD 0.02-0.03 a share a year. These are already in my base case; I have not added a separate "efficiency upside" because the record does not support banking one.

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4718P-0525
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?F1853rd of 54full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

F2551st of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the loop that dominates 2027-2028 free cash flow after distributions is the Norwegian tax-instalment lag. It is balancing over a cycle and strongly reinforcing in the transition year: the cash tax on a high-price year arrives in the first half of the following year, when prices are already lower, so it deepens the cash trough exactly when earnings fall. On my deck the trough lands in FY2028, not H1 2027 as the first set expected, because H1 2027 still carries winter-gas prices; in a sudden normalisation it lands in FY2027 with full force. The other loops are slower: the decline-reinvestment treadmill and the pre-2028 sanction rush (balancing, through supplier inflation) hold volumes flat at a rising cost per barrel; hub consolidation (reinforcing, positive) is what keeps standalone volumes near 380 kboepd rather than DNB Carnegie's 346; the dividend-debt loop is weak in the financials and strong in the valuation; the krone loop is a natural hedge that may fail.

Loop 1: the tax-instalment lag (dominant; reinforcing in the transition year).

  • How it works: Norwegian petroleum tax for an income year is paid in instalments, about half in the second half of that year and the balance, trued up, in the first half of the next. In a rising year cash tax runs below the tax charge (cash flow flattered); in the following falling year cash tax runs above it.
  • Evidence it is running now: Var paid about USD 0.85bn (Q2 2026) and USD 1.14bn (Q3), guides about USD 1.7bn for Q4; Equinor's CFO said H1 2027 tax payments will be higher than H2 2026 (Q3 2026 call); Aker BP guides USD 1.0bn (Q1 2027) and 1.5bn (Q2 2027) against USD 0.5bn in Q3 2026; Harbour's UK schedule front-loads about 60% in-year, so the effect is weaker there.
  • In my base case (derived): FY2026 cash tax USD 4.43bn against a tax charge of USD 6.41bn (+USD 2.0bn to cash); FY2027 USD 6.17bn against 5.71bn (-0.45bn); FY2028 USD 4.87bn against 3.79bn (-1.07bn); FY2029-FY2030 about -0.3bn a year (the D&A-over-capex effect, as tax deductions on past capex have been used). H1 2027 alone carries about USD 3.5bn of residual 2026 tax (range USD 3.2-3.8bn depending on how far the H2 2026 instalments under-estimated the 2026 charge; this is the single largest unknown in the FY2027 cash forecast).
  • Precedent: Equinor's share price fell about 30% within seven months of the 2022 commodity peak as lagged taxes met falling revenue (DNB Carnegie). Var's own 2022-2023: realised gas from USD 204/boe (Q3 2022) to about USD 73/boe (Q1 2026); FactSet free cash flow went from USD 2.16bn (FY2022) to -0.47bn (FY2023).
  • What would strengthen it: a fast normalisation (Hormuz reopening) in H1 2027; what would stall it: prices holding through 2027 (the residual is then paid out of high revenue and the lag simply rolls forward a year).

Loop 2: base decline, reinvestment and the end-2027 sanction rush (balancing; erodes capital efficiency).

  • How it works: an 8-9% base decline (against a 4-6% mature-hub norm) requires about 30-35 kboepd of replacement a year; that needs more than 50 wells a year and about eight sanctions in 2027; every shelf operator is pulling sanctions forward to beat the end-2027 expiry of the 86.9% deduction; subsea contractors are booked into 2027-2028, harsh-environment rigs cost USD 450,000-525,000 a day; so the same dollar buys fewer barrels.
  • Evidence: development capex guidance USD 2.5-2.7bn moving to the top; scheduled reserves and risked resources for 2026-2029 down from 527 to 438 mmboe in nine months; Equinor postponed Wisting and Bay du Nord on supply-chain risk; Aker Solutions' deliveries slipping into 2027.
  • In my model: total capital spending (development, all exploration, abandonment) flat at USD 3.0-3.2bn while standalone volume drifts from 385 to 377 kboepd; capex per barrel produced rises about 5% by FY2030. The balancing force on the cost side is Norges Bank's expectation that petroleum investment falls from 2025 to 2028, which should ease supplier pricing from 2028-2029.

Loop 3: hub consolidation and tie-backs (reinforcing, positive, slow). Each tie-back extends hub life and lowers the threshold for the next (Gjøa to about 2040 via Ofelia, Gjøa Nord and Cerisa; Balder Next subsea delivery cost -30%; selected lead times 30 to 18 months; Balder Phase V sanctioned June 2025, first oil December 2025). This loop is what supports my standalone volume of about 380 kboepd in FY2028 rather than DNB Carnegie's 346; the difference is worth about USD 0.05 of EPS and USD 0.6bn of revenue a year. It does not create growth: the first set's "treadmill" judgement stands.

Loop 4: uptime and unit cost (reinforcing both ways). With a fixed cash cost of about USD 1.55bn standalone, outages cut volume and raise unit cost at the same time (Q3 2026: Johan Castberg, Jotun, Åsgard). Hubs carrying about 40% of output (Balder area and Johan Castberg) make this loop lumpy. A 4-point efficiency recovery towards 95% is worth about 15 kboepd and about USD 0.45/boe of unit cost; a repeat of Q3 2026 for one quarter costs about USD 0.04 of EPS.

Loop 5: dividend, debt and the equity currency (financially weak, reinforcing in valuation). Dividends above free cash flow raise net debt, but the extra interest is small (USD 1bn more debt is about USD 55m of pre-tax interest, about USD 0.01-0.02 a share after tax), so the financial loop barely bites within four years (P-02). The valuation loop is strong: the yield supports a P/E at the 99th percentile of ten years; a high share price was the currency for BlueNord (bought at about 1.24-1.3x NAV with Var at about 1.6x); if FY2028 free cash flow after distributions is visibly negative (my base: -USD 0.85bn), the "dividend cliff" narrative (DNB Carnegie: zero free cash flow yield in 2027-2028) can reverse the multiple and close the M&A route. This loop shapes the share price and the next deal, not my line items.

Loop 6: the krone and oil (historically balancing; may fail). Normally a falling oil price weakens the krone, which cuts USD costs and produces gains on NOK tax balances, a natural hedge. Norges Bank holding 4.5% to June 2027 and Nordea's USD/NOK 9.19 by end-2027 point to a firm krone while oil normalises, the worst combination: about USD 0.31bn of extra USD opex and capex a year per 10% krone strength (about USD 0.07bn after tax) and a one-off tax-translation charge of about USD 0.3bn.

Net shaping of the line items. FY2027: the lag adds about USD 0.45bn to cash tax; decline and supplier costs keep capex at USD 3.15bn; free cash flow after distributions about +USD 0.26bn. FY2028: the lag adds about USD 1.07bn; free cash flow after distributions about -USD 0.85bn, the trough. FY2029-FY2030: the lag fades; free cash flow roughly equals dividends plus coupons at a USD 72-73 Brent deck. Indicators that decide: Q4 2026 cash tax against USD 1.7bn and the first 2027 instalment guidance at the FY2026 results (9 February 2027); the summer-2027 TTF strip; USD/NOK; monthly Norwegian production against about 380 kboepd standalone; the 2027 sanction count and capex guide.

Memo topic 4, Conservatism of guidance and record of delivery: the Rater's score

6a

Guidance conservatism

F4040th of 543 answers
Judgement40C-6840C-7130
Group judgement

Guidance

F4040th of 54

Layer 1 · Guidance

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Specific, honest and not conservative: management's guidance is an excellent set of building blocks and a poor predictor of upside. The company guides every input the model needs (volumes, unit cost, capex by type, quarterly cash tax, hedges, dividend), and gives trading updates before results; it gives no EPS, effective-tax or per-share BlueNord guidance. Its record is to land at the bottom of operating ranges and to raise payouts and long-term ambitions, the reverse of the cohort's habit of guiding low and raising. Adjusted for the change in input forces (start-ups spent, decline 8-9%, supplier inflation, krone strength), the 2026-2028 operating guidance is less conservative than the 2023-2025 guidance was. For the model: use guidance as the structure, set volumes and costs at the bottom of ranges, model the effective tax rate explicitly with a krone sensitivity, and treat informal commentary on Q4 realisations as modest upside to Q4 2026 only.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

F4046th of 54

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: for FY2026, guidance and consensus agree on the inputs, but consensus is above what guidance alone secures because it also embeds a full Q4 volume recovery and Q4 spot prices; for FY2027 the company has not yet guided, and consensus volume (where visible) sits at the top of what management's long-term target implies.

  • FY2026. Guidance: production about 390 kboepd (implying about 410 kboepd in Q4 after 383 for nine months), unit cost about USD 11/boe, development capex USD 2.5-2.7bn, exploration USD 250-300m, abandonment about USD 200m, Q4 cash tax about USD 1.7bn, Q4 dividend USD 400m. Consensus revenue of USD 14.4bn implies Q4 revenue of about USD 4.4bn against Q3's USD 3.57bn; consensus EPS of USD 0.793 against nine-month company EPS of USD 0.58 implies about USD 0.21 for Q4; consensus FCF of USD 3.53bn against USD 2.63bn for nine months implies about USD 0.9bn for Q4 after the tax payment (all derived). The Q4 consensus therefore needs both 410 kboepd and a realised price near USD 115-120/boe. Var's history of landing at the bottom of ranges makes the volume half slightly optimistic; the price half is two-way.
  • FY2027. No formal guidance until the FY2026 results (9 February 2027). Structural guides: average capex about USD 2.5bn a year for 2027-2032 (combined plan), FCF neutrality through the cycle, long-term production about 450 kboepd, a dividend policy of 25-30% of post-tax CFFO through the cycle and a de facto base of USD 350m a quarter. Consensus capex of USD 2.91bn is consistent with the cap plus exploration and BlueNord. Norne has 445 kboepd for 2027 and 450 for 2028, which I judge at the top of the plausible range (about 390 standalone plus about 45 from BlueNord, with base decline only partly offset).
  • Why. Management guides inputs, not outputs; brokers apply decks below spot. The real gap is price, and on dividends, between brokers modelling USD 400m a quarter into 2027 (Morgan Stanley) and USD 350m (Norne).
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

F3047th of 54

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: not conservative; the operating guidance for 2026-2028 is less conservative than in 2023-2025, because the start-up wave that made past guidance achievable is spent while decline, supplier inflation and the krone now work against it.

  • Past: the doubling from 2023 to 2025 was carried by large, discrete start-ups and an acquisition; even so, 2025 landed at the bottom.
  • Now: holding about 390 kboepd standalone requires replacing 30-35 kboepd a year of base decline with more than 50 wells and eight-to-ten sanctions a year; scheduled reserves and risked resources for 2026-2029 fell 17% in nine months; operated efficiency is 91%; Q3 outages hit three hubs at once.
  • Costs: the USD 11/boe guide assumes no further krone strength, while Nordea forecasts USD/NOK falling to 9.19 by end-2027; supplier markets stay tight through 2027-2028.
  • Capex: the USD 2.5bn cap for 2027-2032 follows a history of creep (Balder X, Johan Castberg, 2026 at the top of the range).
  • Where conservatism does exist: in price (impairment testing at a flat USD 65 real), which is not guidance and is applied by every shelf producer.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

A722nd of 54

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: four uncommon features matter for forecasting: the pre-results trading update, the quarterly cash-tax guidance, the dividend's link to BlueNord, and the new tax-translation effect that guidance does not cover.

  • Trading updates (for example 12 October 2026) give production and realised prices about ten days before results, so volume surprises are small on results day and the surprises move into tax, working capital and cash flow (as in Q2 and Q3 2026).
  • Cash tax is guided by quarter (USD 2.5bn for H2 2026 after Q2; about USD 1.7bn for Q4 after Q3), which is the single most useful line for the cash model.
  • The Q2 2026 dividend was paid to standalone holders; the Q3 USD 400m is attributable to shareholders of the combined company pending completion, with the cash consideration adjusted if completion falls after the record date. The per-share dividend therefore depends on the closing date.
  • The functional currency changed from NOK to USD on 1 June 2026; taxes remain computed in NOK; the company does not guide the effective rate.
  • Data cautions: FactSet's enterprise value for Var mixes NOK market value with USD debt (the memo flags it); consensus may mix pre- and post-merger share counts.
  • Next events: FY2026 results 9 February 2027 (likely the first combined guidance and the 2027 dividend framework); the investor-relations team is unchanged.

Case simplicity

Group judgement

Risks

F4040th of 54

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts are predictable in shape, sign and mechanics, and unpredictable in level; and the shape that can be predicted is a decline.

The seven answers fit together as follows. The earlier sets are congruent (D-01), and the base case was built to need only strengths Var has shown (D-02), so there is little risk that the forecasts are wrong about the company. The risk is that they are wrong about one external number: the realised price explains three-quarters of the EPS path from the FY2026 peak (D-03) and produces a cone for FY2027 EPS of USD 0.27 to 0.81 around 0.51 (D-04). Nothing the company controls is large enough to narrow it: uptime, unit cost and synergies together are worth a few cents. What rescues the case from being unforecastable is that the cone has a fixed geometry. EPS does not rise in FY2027 and falls in FY2028 on every path; FY2028 distributions exceed FY2028 free cash flow on every path; the balance sheet holds on every path; about USD 3.4-4.5bn of tax leaves in H1 2027 on every path; and consensus EPS has to come down to the right share count on every path (I-02). Disclosure is good enough to watch all of this in close to real time (D-05), and the chain from price to EBITDA is as clear as any in the market, although the last step to EPS is cluttered by five items the company does not bridge (D-06). The share price is three-quarters a sector position with a beta of 1.25, carrying the most stretched multiple in its cohort for reasons that are passive, not earned (D-07).

For the memo: treat the level of FY2027-FY2028 earnings as a range and the direction as a near-certainty. An investment case in this stock over 18 months is a view that gas and oil prices stay near the levels of late 2026 for longer than the curve and the brokers assume, expressed through the most expensive vehicle in the sector, and even if that view is right earnings per share do not grow. I have moved the weights a little towards the downside since the second set wrote (30 bear, 50 base, 20 bull), on the evidence of the last three weeks. The three inputs I changed correct the per-share arithmetic in the model and lower its EPS by about 5% and its return at the guideline multiple by about two points; they do not change the view. The indicators that will resolve most of the uncertainty are dated: the Q4 trading update in late January, the guidance and dividend framework on 9 February, the end-of-winter storage level in March, and the first consolidated quarter in late April.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

B6410th of 54

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: narrow (one product family, one fiscal regime, one cost currency), which makes the operating model predictable and the earnings unpredictable; BlueNord widens the perimeter slightly and adds a second operator and regime.

  • Today: upstream oil and gas only (65% liquids, 35% gas); Norway only; about 200 licences, a third operated, 57 fields across four hubs; the Balder area and Johan Castberg are about 40% of production; Eni buys about 79% of sales.
  • After BlueNord: a fifth hub in Denmark (36.8% non-operated DUC, operated by TotalEnergies; Tyra plus Dan, Gorm and Halfdan base assets), a second fiscal regime, Danish krone taxes, and more gas.
  • Predictability: volumes, costs and capex are trackable (monthly Norwegian production data, quarterly trading updates, specific guidance lines); earnings are dominated by price, krone and tax translation, which the company does not hedge. Concentration on a few hubs makes unplanned outages a single-point risk (Q3 2026).
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

C7221st of 54

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the base case is plausible because it was built to need only what Var has already shown it can do: integrate an acquisition, refinance cheaply, start up small tie-backs, and pay a dividend through a year in which free cash flow does not cover it. The company's own targets are a different matter: a 450 kboepd plateau, unit cost back at USD 10 and a USD 2.5bn capex cap each need a strength Var has not yet demonstrated (sustained uptime above 95%; small-project delivery repeated for several years; being a non-operating partner in a second country). The gap between the two matters little to the next 18 months' earnings, because the undemonstrated strengths are worth a few cents a share; it matters a great deal to whether the story can improve, because every source of upside to the base case, other than price, lies on the undemonstrated side.

Three observations that turn the table into a judgement.

  • The nature of delivery required has changed, and the record covers the old kind better than the new. The doubling of 2023-2025 came from two very large projects (one of them, Johan Castberg, built and operated by Equinor) and one acquisition. Holding the plateau in 2027-2028 needs many small things done reliably every year: more than 50 wells, about eight sanctions, compressors that restart. That is the competence Aker BP has shown for a decade (its last ten projects on schedule and 2% below budget) and Var has shown for one year. The precedent closest to Var's chief executive points the same way: Lundin's assets produced about 10% below target after their sale.
  • The early signs on the new kind of delivery are mixed, not bad. For: 2026's small projects are on time. Against: the upgraded compressor on Jotun, first planned for 2026, is now "2027 or 2028", and the retirement of the old Balder production unit in 2028 (USD 117m a year net to Var, the only identified cost step in the plan) depends on it and has not yet been sanctioned; scheduled reserves and risked resources for 2026-2029 fell from 527 to 438 mmboe in nine months, which the company has not explained (part may simply be projects that started up and left the category; the files do not say).
  • The financial stakes of the capability gap are small; the narrative stakes are not. If Var acquires none of the missing strengths, the base case loses perhaps USD 0.01-0.02 of EPS. If it acquires all of them (95% uptime, USD 10 cost), it gains USD 0.03-0.04. Neither moves a forecast whose cone is USD 0.27 to 0.81 (D-04). But the investment case would need the story to get better, and the only company-made improvements available are the undemonstrated ones.

The company's own targets, each rated for plausibility on the record. BlueNord synergies met or beaten: high. Dividend of USD 350m a quarter held on the base deck: high. Capex cap of about USD 2.5bn a year: medium (small projects, most of any overrun borne by the state, but a sanction rush before the end-2027 tax deadline into a booked supplier market). Unit cost back to USD 10: low before 2029 (it needs the Balder retirement, worth about USD 0.8/boe, and 95% uptime, worth about USD 0.45). A 450 kboepd plateau: low (it needs about 405 kboepd standalone, a level reached in one quarter and one month of 2026, before a year of 8-9% decline). The AlphaSense report written for this question reaches the same ranking: high confidence on synergies, medium on cost and uptime, low on the plateau, the capex cap and the dividend under normalised prices.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

F2547th of 54

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: wide in level, narrow in shape. FY2027 EPS spans about USD 0.27 to 0.81 around a base of 0.51 (47% below to 58% above), which is too wide for the level to be called predictable. But the cone opens late: the first two of the six quarters are largely fixed already. And three features hold across the whole cone: EPS does not rise in FY2027 and falls in FY2028 on every path; free cash flow fails to cover distributions in FY2028 on every path; and the balance sheet is not threatened on any path. I put the skew modestly to the downside, a little more than the second set did, because of what has happened to prices since 22 October. The case is therefore predictable in its direction and its mechanics and unpredictable in its magnitude, and the predictable direction is down.

The cone opens late: what is already fixed in each quarter (derived).

  • Q4 2026 (reported 9 February 2027; base revenue about 4,150, EPS about 0.22). Crude cargoes are priced one to two months before delivery, so most of the quarter's oil price is set; about 30% of gas is fixed at about USD 82/boe; production is visible monthly; cash tax is guided at about USD 1.7bn. Revenue is within about 4% either way. EPS is wider, about 0.17 to 0.27, and most of that width is not operational: a 2.5% move in the krone against the dollar revalues the tax payable by about USD 0.09bn, USD 0.03 a share.
  • Q1 2027 (base revenue about 4,015). The same 30% of gas is fixed; 40-50% of BlueNord's volumes are hedged (oil at about USD 65/bbl, gas at about EUR 31/MWh, collars capped at USD 74 and EUR 37.5); the rest is winter spot. Within about 12% either way.
  • Q2 2027 to Q1 2028 (base revenue about 12,590 for the four quarters). Open to the full price range: 25-30% either way.
  • The first two quarters are 39% of the six quarters' base revenue. So about two-fifths of the 18 months is narrow and three-fifths is wide.

Probability about 30 in 100 (20) about 50 in 100 (55) about 20 in 100 (25)
Brent, USD/bbl and TTF, EUR/MWh: FY2027; FY2028 65 and 35; 60 and 28 81 and 51.5; 72 and 35 95 and 75; 85 and 55
EPS: FY2027; FY2028 about 0.27; 0.17 0.513; 0.339 about 0.81; 0.61
Free cash flow, cash basis: FY2027; FY2028 about 0; 0.5-0.75bn 1.9bn; 0.7bn about 4.2bn; 1.3bn
After dividends and hybrid coupons: FY2027; FY2028 about -1.6bn; -0.3 to -0.8bn after a cut +0.3bn; -0.85bn about +2.5bn; about -0.3bn
Dividend Cut by 20-30% at the FY2027 results (February 2028) USD 350m a quarter held Extraordinary payments continue
Net debt with hybrids, times EBITDA, end-2028 about 1.1x 0.65x about 0.3x

Probability-weighted FY2027 EPS is about 0.50 on my weights (derived), 3% below the base case and 16% below consensus of 0.596.

Why I move ten points of probability from the top to the bottom. The second set's base case assumed Brent of about USD 100 in Q4 2026 and USD 90 in Q1 2027. Since Brent's USD 111 on 22 October it has fallen to USD 91.5 (Deutsche Bank, 11 November) as fears of escalation in the Middle East receded; the physical North Sea market has loosened sharply (dated Brent below January futures, 36 cargoes unsold in ten sessions, Equinor's Oseberg premium cut without drawing a bid; Morgan Stanley, 9 November); TTF spot is about USD 28/mmbtu (DNB Carnegie, 10 November) after Morgan Stanley's "TTF EUR 101 and climbing" on 30 October; Qatari and Emirati LNG exports are expected back in Q1 2027 and flows are being restored through other routes. And the base rate is against the curve: spot gas ended the winter 10-60% below the forward curve in three of the last five winters (DNB Carnegie). Against that, storage is 73-75% full, the lowest since 2011, Russian LNG is banned from 1 January 2027, and a cold winter would drain storage towards 15%. The first step of the normalisation is arriving a quarter earlier than the base case has it; that is a reason to shift the weights, not yet the base inputs, because the base deck for Q1 2027 (Brent 90, TTF 70) is at or below today's spot.

Other sources of skew, beyond the price weights.

  • Operations skew down. Uptime has a ceiling (97% in the best quarter) and no floor: the base case assumes about 94-95%, so the upside is about 8 kboepd and a repeat of Q3 2026 costs 15-25 kboepd for a quarter (about USD 0.02-0.04 of EPS).
  • Contracts and hedges truncate both tails in the near quarters, the upper one more. The fixed 30% of gas and BlueNord's collars cap what a spike can add; on the way down, month-ahead gas indexation pays Var about USD 4-5/boe above spot and the 78% tax absorbs most of the fall.
  • Tax translation skews slightly down for the year. Nordea has the krone weaker by mid-2027 (USD/NOK 9.82, a credit) and stronger by end-2027 (9.19, a charge); net, about one point on the FY2027 tax rate, which the base case already carries.
  • Cash is more skewed than earnings. In the bear case the first year takes the lower price and the full USD 3.6bn of tax on 2026 income, so free cash flow falls to about zero; in the bull case the windfall of 2027 is taxed in 2028. The equity consequence is one-sided: only the bear case leads to a dividend cut, and it would be announced in February 2028, inside the 18 months.

What holds across the whole cone, which is where the predictability lies.

  1. EPS does not rise. FY2027 is 0.27, 0.51 or 0.81 against 0.80 in FY2026; FY2028 is below FY2027 in all three cases (0.17, 0.34, 0.61). Even the path on which spot prices persist through 2027 produces no growth, because 10% more shares, higher finance costs and a normal tax rate absorb it.
  2. From Q2 2027, revenue is below the same quarter a year earlier in the base and bear cases (base: -8%, -14%, -25%, -26% for Q2 2027 to Q1 2028) and about level in the bull case.
  3. FY2028 distributions are not covered by FY2028 free cash flow in any case: by about USD 0.85bn in the base case, by USD 0.3bn in the bull case (the tax on 2027), and by USD 0.3-0.8bn in the bear case even after a cut.
  4. Leverage stays at or below about 1.1 times EBITDA with hybrids counted as debt, against a ceiling of 1.3 times without them, so there is no path in the cone to a forced equity or asset event.
  5. About USD 3.4-4.5bn of cash tax leaves in H1 2027 whatever happens to prices, two-fifths in Q1 and three-fifths in Q2; if there is a quarter of negative free cash flow in the 18 months, it is Q2 2027.

The precedent for how such a cone resolves. After the 2022 peak: Var's revenue fell 30% in FY2023, EBIT 43%, and FactSet free cash flow went from +USD 2.2bn to -USD 0.5bn; Equinor's free cash flow fell from about USD 29bn to about USD 7bn and its shares fell about 30% within seven months; in Q2 2023, the quarter of two instalments under the old schedule, free cash flow was negative at Var (-USD 0.66bn), Aker BP and Equinor together. DNB Carnegie then projected cash tax for H2 2023 54% lower at Var, and cash flow recovered. The pattern is a sharp, short cash trough that the equity market prices ahead of time and that the balance sheets survive. That is the shape of the bear case here.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

A881st of 54

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: very helpful, and better than the case deserves on its fundamentals. Between public daily prices, monthly government production data, a trading update about ten days before each result and quarterly guidance that includes cash tax with a price sensitivity, an outside analyst can know a quarter's revenue and EBITDA to within a few percent before it is reported, and can see every one of the five things that will decide the next six months on a known date. What disclosure does not cover is everything between EBITDA and EPS, and that is exactly where the last two quarters' surprises have been.

The calendar to mid-May 2027, and what each item tests.

When What is published What it tests
Daily Brent, North Sea crude differentials, TTF, USD/NOK The driver of three-quarters of the EPS path; the krone gives the quarter's tax translation in advance (payable USD 3.3bn, about USD 0.03bn per 1%)
Monthly, about three weeks after month-end Norwegian Offshore Directorate production by field (Var's reported volumes run 4-5% above it, because Var counts fuel, flare and injected gas; DNB Carnegie) Q4 2026 at 395-410 kboepd; outages at Jotun, Johan Castberg, Åsgard and Ormen Lange
Monthly until completion BlueNord's preliminary production Tyra at 24-29 kboepd and the base assets at 18-20, the Q4 guidance
Around year-end 2026 BlueNord completion; refinancing of its 7.875% bond (holders may put it at 101% on the change of control), its loan and its 12% hybrid The share count from 1 January; the first financing synergy
Late January 2027 Q4 trading update: production and realised prices by product Whether 2026 guidance of "about 390 kboepd" was met (it needs about 410 in Q4; the base case has 395)
9 February 2027 FY2026 results; the first combined 2027 guidance (production, unit cost, capex, exploration, abandonment); cash tax for Q1 and Q2 2027; the Q4 dividend and the 2027 dividend framework; reserves; whether the Balder production unit's retirement is sanctioned; the Jotun compressor date Most of I-01 on one day
By end-March 2027 European gas storage at the end of winter (about 15% in a cold case to 26% in a mild one) The summer-2027 TTF strip, which sets the FY2027 deck
Mid- and late April 2027 Q1 trading update; Q1 results, the first consolidated quarter Purchase-price depreciation per barrel, the treatment of BlueNord's hedges, finance costs with BlueNord's debt, EPS on 2,744.8m shares, Q1 cash tax paid

Why I rate this highly.

  • The dominant driver is observable daily and the company tells you how it passes through: fixed and indexed shares of gas with average prices, the crude premium expected for the next quarter, hedges, and a table of cash tax against price.
  • Volume, the main company-controlled driver, is observable monthly from an independent source, and the trading update removes most of what is left. The proof is in the record: EBITDA was within 1-1.4% of consensus in both Q2 and Q3 2026.
  • The guidance lines are the model's lines: production, unit cost, capex by type, cash tax by quarter, dividend by quarter. Aker BP matches Var on cash-tax guidance and exceeds it on currency (it discloses hedge cover and rates); Equinor gives less. Var is in the top half of its cohort for trackability.

What cannot be monitored, and why it matters for these six months in particular.

  • The effective tax rate. No guidance and no sensitivity from the company, although the item swung net income by +74% and -35% against consensus in Q2 and Q3. It can be estimated from the krone, but the market has not been doing so.
  • The bridge from BlueNord to EPS. No per-share bridge, no purchase-price allocation, no statement on the hedges, until late April 2027. For four months after completion, the combined company's EPS will be a matter of each analyst's assumptions.
  • Outages by asset in barrels, the decline rate by hub, and a project-by-project volume bridge for 2027. The plateau claim cannot be audited from outside; only its result can be watched.
  • Whether monthly Danish data continue after BlueNord is absorbed. Not stated.
  • The terms of sales to Eni and the headroom on receivables factoring (USD 349m sold at Q3). Not disclosed.

The use of this for the memo. The thesis is falsifiable on dates. If Q4 volumes reach about 410, the 2027 guide is at or above 440 kboepd with unit cost at USD 10-11, and the dividend framework holds USD 400m a quarter, the bull narrative gains three months of support whatever our view of FY2028. If two of those three fail, the bear narrative takes over early. Either way the reader will know by mid-February.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

B5811th of 54

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the trace is exceptionally clear as far as EBITDA and breaks down between EBITDA and EPS. The external chain, from Brent, TTF and the krone to earnings, is short, quantifiable and almost free of judgement. The internal chain has a different problem: what the company says it will achieve is stated in barrels, reserves, sanctions and dividends, never in earnings, and when the analyst builds the trace to EPS it shows that everything the company promises, added together, is worth less than a USD 5/boe move in the price.

The transmission, as the memo can use it (derived by the first and second sets; FY2027-FY2028 basis, 2,744.8m shares).

Driver Move EBITDA Net income EPS
Realised price USD 10/boe 1.55bn 0.32bn 0.117
Volume 10 kboepd 0.16bn (EBIT) 0.035bn 0.013
Unit production cost USD 1/boe 0.155bn 0.033bn 0.012
Effective tax rate 1 point - 0.072bn 0.026
Krone 5% stronger -0.075bn of opex a year, and -0.08bn of capex -0.035bn a year, plus about -0.17bn once from revaluing the tax payable -0.013 a year, plus about -0.06 once
Tax-payment timing - - none none on EPS; on cash +2.0bn in FY2026, -0.45bn in FY2027, -1.1bn in FY2028

The external chain: clear, with three lags that make quarters noisy and years clean.

  • Price to revenue. Oil is sold at dated Brent plus a differential (USD 6/bbl in Q2 and Q3 2026; the base case has USD 2-3 in 2027), priced one to two months ahead. Gas is a disclosed basket: in Q3 2026, 29% fixed or year-ahead at about USD 87/boe and 25% month-ahead, which left the realised price USD 18/boe below spot in a rising market and will leave it above spot in a falling one. BlueNord's volumes come with hedges at prices well below today's. Each of these moves a quarter's realisation by USD 3-5/boe (about USD 0.01 of EPS) and nets out over a year.
  • Revenue to net income. No royalties; about USD 14/boe of cash cost that does not move with price; 78% tax on the margin. That is the whole chain.
  • The krone. About 70% of opex and 60% of development capex are in kroner, unhedged; and since 1 June 2026 the tax payable is revalued through the tax line.
  • Suppliers and the fiscal regime. Cost inflation reaches cash more than earnings (the state bears 78-87% of capital spending) and reaches EPS only later through depreciation; the end-2027 expiry of the temporary tax rules changes project returns, not 2027-2028 earnings.
  • The cash chain has its own lag, six to ten months between the tax charge and its payment, which the company quantifies.

The internal chain: from what the company says to the line it lands on.

What the company says it will achieve The line it lands on Traceable to EPS? Worth (derived)
About 450 kboepd long term with BlueNord Volume, revenue In principle; no annual bridge by project and no decline rate, so not auditable Against the base case's 425-428: USD 0.03
Unit cost of USD 10-11/boe for the combined group Production cost Yes USD 0.01 per USD 1
Retirement of the Balder production unit in 2028, USD 130m gross a year Production cost from mid-2028 Yes USD 0.01
Capex of about USD 2.5bn a year on average for 2027-2032 Cash; depreciation later To cash, not to EPS -
About eight sanctions and more than 100% organic reserve replacement in 2027 Reserve life No, not within four years -
BlueNord synergies of USD 250-300m after tax over 2027-2032 Mostly net financial items Yes, once the refinancing is done USD 0.016 a year
BlueNord "accretive to production, reserves, cash flow from operations and free cash flow per share" - No EPS bridge given; on the base case BlueNord adds 1-3% to EPS (about USD 0.10-0.15bn of net income for 9.95% more shares; derived) USD 0.005-0.01
Dividend of USD 350m a quarter as a base; 25-30% of cash flow from operations after tax through the cycle Cash Not an earnings item DPS 0.51

Where the trace fails: five items between EBITDA and EPS that the company does not bridge. Depreciation after BlueNord's purchase accounting (unknown until April 2027); net financial items, now about USD 480m a year including about USD 190m of non-cash accretion, where interest was largely capitalised until 2025; the tax translation; hybrid coupons of about USD 110m; and the share count. Together they turn FY2027 EBITDA of USD 11.5bn into net income for ordinary shareholders of USD 1.4bn. The evidence that the market's trace is broken here, not just ours: FactSet's FY2027 consensus pre-tax profit (7,122) is above consensus EBIT (6,875), which cannot be right for a company with these finance costs; consensus EPS sits on mixed share counts; and reported net income missed consensus by 35% in Q3 2026 with EBITDA 1% away from it.

The judgement in one sentence for the memo's topic on simplicity. This is a simple case to trace and a hard one to bank: one external number sets most of the outcome through a short and well-disclosed chain, the company's own promises barely register in EPS, and the part of the chain that is opaque is the part that turns operating profit into earnings per share.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D2236th of 54

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: Var is the most sentiment-exposed name in its cohort: it carries the highest P/E percentile, a dividend-defined valuation, an energy-hedge role in portfolios, and a visible bear campaign; the trigger set is wide and largely outside the company.

  • Sector de-rating on a Brent read under USD 85. The weekly environment note says Brent under USD 85 "removes the reason for holding the energy hedge at all" and would remove the energy and Norway Overweights; the industry and Norway notes say the same. Var is the high-multiple end of a group that would de-rate together.
  • The dividend-cliff narrative. DNB Carnegie (Sell, NOK 46) argues free cash flow yield is about zero in 2027-2028; Norne notes a USD 350m quarterly dividend exceeds its 2027 EPS at USD 75 Brent; once the market focuses on 2028 FCF of about USD 0.95bn against at least USD 1.4bn of dividends, the yield can be repriced quickly.
  • Peer read-across. UK producers' payouts break first (Ithaca below USD 70; Harbour suspends buybacks at USD 60); a dividend cut anywhere in European E&P would be read across to the highest-yield name.
  • Discount rate. Morgan Stanley's NOK 56 rests on a 9% cost of equity; its bear case of NOK 41 uses 9.5%; the US ten-year above 5% and ECB hikes push the other way.
  • Momentum unwinding. The shares fell about 12% from NOK 56.22 (20 October) to NOK 49.6 and sit below the 50-day average (53.4), though still 10.3% above the 200-day; momentum funds hold a stock that has risen 63.5% in twelve months.
  • Ownership events. An Eni placing, or index changes around the free float rising to about 43%.

C-69

F3544th of 54

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: a consistent pattern of small misses on volumes and costs, repeated misses on cash flow, and random outcomes on net income driven by currency and tax; beats come from price, currency and distributions, not from operations.

  • Last four quarters. Q4 2025: net profit USD 25m, 77% below consensus (taxes). Q1 2026: CFFO hit by a USD 525m working-capital build (32% below consensus per the memo). Q2 2026: production 376 against 386 kboepd consensus (-3%); revenue +4%; EBITDA -1%; net profit +74% (USD 268m FX gain, 65% tax); CFFO -16%; unit cost USD 11.2 against 10.4. Q3 2026: production 369 against about 388 (-5%); EBITDAX -1.4%; net income -35%; EPS USD 0.11 against 0.19 (-42%); CFFO -24%; FCF -34% (USD 758m against about 1.15-1.2bn).
  • Three years. 2024 production within range; 2025 at the bottom; 2026 cut to the bottom; 2025 cost met, 2026 cost missed; dividends always met or raised; Neptune synergies doubled.
  • Five years (since the February 2022 IPO). Jefferies: the company "historically frustrated the market post-IPO with near-term production downgrades and cost revisions"; Balder X first oil slipped from 2022 to June 2025 with a doubling of cost; Johan Castberg (Equinor-operated) overran.
  • Comparable conditions. In the 2026 high-price year, price hides the volume misses at the revenue line, and the misses appear in cash and below EBITDA; that is the pattern to expect again if prices stay high.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F3848th of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the forecasts deserve high weight for their structure and low weight for their level. Var reliably delivers its capital plan, its distributions and its transaction synergies, and reliably under-delivers on in-year volumes, unit cost and quarterly cash conversion, while its net income is a random variable driven by currency and tax. So the forecasts should be leaned down on volumes (2-4% below company targets), up on unit cost (USD 0.3-0.5/boe above guidance), to the top of capex ranges, down on quarterly cash flow, up on dividends relative to the stated policy, and not leaned on price, which is two-way. I have built those leans into the base case; I would apply a further risk weighting of about USD 0.2bn a year to free cash flow but not to EPS.

The record, by line.

How much weight, by horizon.

  • Next six months (Q4 2026, Q1 2027): high weight on volume (trading update about ten days before results; Q4 recovery to about 395-410 kboepd), on capex and on cash tax (guided by quarter); low weight on reported EPS (a 5% krone move changes the quarter's tax charge by about USD 0.15bn, more than the whole quarter's operating miss in Q3 2026).
  • 6 to 18 months: the BlueNord integration and the 2027 dividend framework are the new tests; the Neptune precedent supports leaning synergies up, Tyra's history (4-6 week compressor and electrical outages; Q3 2026 BlueNord production 4% below guided midpoint) supports leaning BlueNord volume down by 1-2 kboepd.
  • 18 months to four years: weight falls sharply; the price deck dominates and the company gives no EPS guidance.

Direction of lean already in my base case. Volume 428 kboepd in FY2027 against Norne's 445 and the company's "about 450" long-term target (-4% to -5%); standalone unit cost USD 11.2/boe in FY2027 against "about 11" guidance and the USD 10 aspiration; capital spending at the top of guidance (USD 2.65bn development) plus all exploration and abandonment; D&A above consensus. Not leaned: price, and net income's tax noise (I use 79% in FY2027, 1 point above the marginal rate, for the expected krone strength and partial deductibility of finance costs).

Peers' behaviour, for context. Aker BP has landed at or above the top of its production range for three years and raised the low end in 2026; Harbour upgraded twice in 2026; DNO raised North Sea guidance; BlueNord's base assets met or beat guidance for 17 consecutive quarters. Var is the exception in a cohort that guides low and beats, so the sector's "consensus is a floor" rule applies to Var's estimates only through price, never through operations.

Where I differ from the first set. None in substance; I add that the cash-flow misses have been timing-driven (working capital, tax instalments, liftings) and largely reverse within a year, so the right lean on annual free cash flow is modest (about USD 0.2bn) even though the quarterly misses have been 16-34%.

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

F1852nd of 54

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

A peak-to-plateau earnings path whose per-share outcome is worse than its profit outcome, whose cash trough comes a year after its earnings fall, and whose dividend is affordable on a normal deck but not on a fast fall. The forecasts rest on one dominant driver (the price deck) acting through a larger fixed-cost base than the company has ever had (USD 38/boe ahead of EBIT), so a 29% fall in realised price from FY2026 to FY2028 becomes a 38% fall in EBIT and, after 10% dilution, fixed hybrid coupons and finance costs more than double FY2025's, a 58% fall in EPS (USD 0.80 to 0.34). Cost programmes cannot change that shape: after the state's 78% share and the krone, they are worth about USD 0.02-0.03 a share by FY2029. The tax-instalment lag moves about USD 2bn of cash into FY2026 and takes back about USD 1.5bn across FY2027-FY2028, so free cash flow after distributions is positive in FY2027 and about -USD 0.85bn in FY2028; once the lag clears, the USD 350m quarterly dividend is roughly covered at about USD 75 Brent and the balance sheet stays at about 0.5x (0.7x with hybrids). The company's record says lean volumes and cost against it, capex to the top, dividends and synergies in its favour; that is built in. Against consensus, my operating lines are at or slightly above (FY2027 revenue +6%, EBIT +12%, because I keep winter-gas prices in H1 2027 and higher D&A offsets less), my net income is in line (within 4%), and my EPS is 14% (FY2027) and 17% (FY2028) below, because consensus EPS appears to sit on a pre-merger share basis without full hybrid deductions. For the third and fourth sets, the most predictable parts of the case are the structure and the per-share re-basing; the least predictable are the price level, the H1 2027 tax true-up and the quarterly tax charge under the new currency regime.

Read: my net income is within 4% of consensus in both years, but consensus EPS sits between the pre-merger and post-merger bases. FY2028 consensus EPS (0.407) almost exactly equals consensus net income over 2,496.4m shares with no hybrid deduction (0.413). On the named brokers: DNB Carnegie uses 2,496.4m shares and standalone volumes (376 and 346 kboepd) and has EPS of 0.53 and 0.35; Norne uses 2,744.8m and 445-450 kboepd and has 0.48 and 0.41; Morgan Stanley 0.58 and 0.28; Fearnley's 0.915 and 0.887 (with a Sell rating) is an outlier on deck or basis. The FY2027 broker range (0.47-0.915) is mostly price deck, but perhaps 0.05-0.06 of it is basis. The first set suspected share-count mixing in FY2027; the evidence is stronger for FY2028. Once BlueNord closes and FactSet's contributors re-base, the FY2027-FY2028 consensus EPS lines should fall by roughly this amount without any change in view, which matters for the fourth set's revision analysis.

Tax. History: reported rates of 75.3% (FY2021), 84.0%, 81.8%, 90.1% (FY2024, FX loss year), 80.3% (FY2025); nine months of 2026 74% (Q2 65%, Q3 85%). Forecast: 79.0% (FY2027), 78.5%, 78.0%, 78.0%. Drivers above 78%: krone strength revaluing NOK tax payable under the USD functional currency, and partial deductibility of finance costs against the 71.8% special tax. Drivers below: BlueNord's Danish income (64% hydrocarbon tax) and its tax-loss carry-forwards (Chapter 3a USD 625.1m; deferred tax assets USD 593m), which DNB Carnegie expects to help cash tax mainly in 2027. Sensitivities: 1 point of effective rate on FY2027 pre-tax profit is about USD 72m, USD 0.026 a share; a 10% krone move is a one-off USD 0.3bn of tax charge (USD 0.11 a share) in the quarter of the move. DNO's 106.3% FY2025 rate shows how a translation charge can dominate a smaller pre-tax profit; at FY2028 pre-tax profit of USD 4.8bn, a 10% krone move would add about 6 points to the year's rate.

Interest and other finance items. See P-02: USD -480m (FY2027) to -440m (FY2030), of which about USD 190-200m a year is non-cash accretion on about USD 5.3bn of abandonment obligations (Var USD 3.889bn, BlueNord USD 1.374bn). EUR-denominated senior and hybrid notes under a USD functional currency will create FX gains and losses in finance items; I forecast none. FY2026 net financial items of about USD -23m include USD 268m of Q2 FX gains.

One-offs and impairments. None forecast. Risks: a 25% cut in long-term price assumptions would add about USD 2.1bn of impairment (company sensitivity, Q3 2026); dry wells (USD 173m in FY2025) sit in my D&A line at about USD 150m a year; purchase-price accounting on BlueNord could create goodwill (Pandion added USD 104m of goodwill).

Minorities and associates. None.

Share count. 248.4m BlueNord consideration shares (non-cash) from completion; employee share programme issuance small (USD 6.9m net in 2026 to date); no buybacks (the thin ordinary equity and Eni's 57.33% post-completion stake make buybacks unlikely; the first set agreed). An Eni sell-down would not change the count.

Judgement: my base case is a two-step normalisation: winter 2026-27 keeps H1 2027 strong (Brent about USD 86, TTF about EUR 60 on average), then the 2027-2028 LNG wave and a likely easing of the Hormuz premium bring Brent to about USD 72 and TTF to EUR 30-35 from 2028. I give this path about 55 in 100; a bull path (spot persisting through 2027) about 25 in 100; a bear path (sudden normalisation from Q1 2027, Brent to USD 60-65) about 20 in 100.

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F4253rd of 54

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: modest scope from a few operational areas (uptime, unit cost, realisation, tax normalisation, BlueNord financing), worth perhaps USD 0.05-0.10 a share a year in total (derived), which is less than one USD 10/boe move in price; it is congruent with the strategy but rests on execution that slipped in 2026.

  • Uptime and volume: restoring production efficiency from 91% towards the 94-97% seen in H1 and Q1 2026, and holding standalone production at 390-400 kboepd rather than the 383 kboepd of the first nine months, is worth about 10-20 kboepd, about USD 0.015-0.03 a share (derived).
  • Unit cost back to USD 10/boe: about USD 0.013 a share (derived).
  • Crude realisation: an extra USD 3/bbl on liquids is worth about USD 0.025 a share (derived); not controllable.
  • Tax: normalising the effective rate from 85% to the 78% marginal rate is worth more than any operating lever for a given quarter; it reverses with the krone.
  • BlueNord: financing synergies (BNOR18 at 7.875% refinanced under an investment-grade issuer) and overheads; USD 250-300m post-tax over six years.
  • Peer precedents: Aker BP's alliance model shows the shelf can deliver lower costs and early start-ups; Var's own Neptune integration doubled its synergy target. Var has not yet shown Aker BP-style uptime.
  • Strategy congruence: the project factory, BEST and hub consolidation target exactly these levers.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F1852nd of 54

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: the volume engine has stopped (growth from +51% to about zero in two quarters) and the price engine has peaked; reported revenue growth still looks strong only because of base effects and price.

  • Volume: production growth year on year was +51% in Q1 2026 (406 kboepd), +31% in Q2 (376) and about 0% in Q3 (369 against 370). The engine of 2024-2026 (Neptune plus the start-up wave) is spent; 2027's only step is BlueNord.
  • Revenue: +45%, +103% and +69% year on year in Q1, Q2 and Q3 2026, but -4% sequentially in Q3; consensus then has -10.5% (2027) and -16.3% (2028).
  • Input force: Brent rose about 46% after late February 2026 to USD 104-111; the weekly note (6 November) carries an undated headline of oil dipping on peace hopes; Deutsche Bank expects normalisation from Q1 2027; 68% of surveyed investors expect Brent below USD 100 at end-2026.
  • The company's own normal rate: there is none; growth has always been acquired or project-step driven (about 80% via M&A), so "below normal" means "no step left".
