Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 12 November 2026, 19:40 to 20:09 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Var Energi (VAR-NO): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect

Written 2026-11-12 by the Investment Analyst answering the third and fourth sets, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer

  • Units and bases. Money in USD millions unless stated; per-share figures in USD. Price NOK 49.6 (2026-11-11), USD 5.20 at the financials block's NOK 9.54 per USD. "The base case" is the second set's base case; I keep all of its operating inputs (price deck, volumes, margins, depreciation, capital spending, dividends). EPS is on the company's own definition: net income less hybrid coupons, over the shares in issue (2,496.4m in 2026; 2,744.8m from BlueNord completion, expected around year-end 2026). Consensus is the FactSet median at 2026-11-11.
  • "(derived)" marks my own arithmetic on the files' figures; probabilities are my judgement.
  • I change three forecast inputs (file inputs-changed.md). All three correct how the Financial Analyst's model carries the per-share mechanics; none changes the view of the business. They are explained in the next section.
  • Free cash flow comes on three definitions; use the first for every statement about dividend cover. (1) Cash basis, the second set's: operating cash flow after cash tax actually paid and cash interest, less all capital spending including exploration and abandonment, less leases. (2) FactSet and consensus: operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex, about USD 0.4-0.6bn a year higher than (1). (3) The model's "equity free cash flow": EBITDA less the tax charge (not the cash tax), less all net financial items, less capital spending, leases and the change in working capital (derived; it reproduces the model's five figures to within USD 1m).
  • Evidence limits. The narrative evidence rests on the research of four covering houses (Morgan Stanley, Jefferies, DNB Carnegie, Norne) out of at least thirteen; FactSet's broker table adds eight others' numbers but not their arguments. The files hold no investor or social-media commentary at all. I have not relied on AlphaSense statements that the files' own numbers contradict; the main one is the claim that consensus underestimates the tax-payment lag (I-03).

The gaps between the second set's cross-check and the model: each one explained

The model summary flags five gaps: FY2027 EPS, and free cash flow in FY2026, FY2027, FY2028 and FY2030. The EPS gap shows three inputs to be wrong for this model; the free cash flow gaps show no input to be wrong, but a limit of the model that the memo must read around.

EPS: the model's figure is the net result of two opposite errors.

  1. The model does not deduct hybrid coupons (stated on the summary: "Not modelled: minority interests and hybrid bond coupons"). The company's basic EPS does. The coupons are about 97, 110, 110, 92 and 90 for FY2026 to FY2030, USD 0.033-0.040 a share. This lifts model EPS above the cross-check.
  2. The model's share count is too high. The second set entered the EUR 750m hybrid issued in April 2026 (USD 889m with USD 7m of employee shares) on the line "Buybacks less new shares issued for cash", so that net debt would reconcile. The model treats that line as ordinary shares sold for cash at the current price, about 171m new shares from Q4 2026. The evidence is the model's own output: net income of 1,038.5, 1,024.8 and 968.2 over EPS of 0.356, 0.352 and 0.332 implies about 2,915m shares in FY2028-FY2030, which is 2,744.8m plus 171m; and FY2026's implied average of about 2,540m is three quarters at 2,496.4m and one at about 2,667m (derived). Separately, the model phases the 248.4m BlueNord shares evenly through the four quarters of FY2027 (its FY2027 EPS of 0.540 matches quarterly counts of about 2,729m, 2,791m, 2,853m and 2,916m; derived), although the shares are issued at completion and so should count for the whole of FY2027. Both lower model EPS.
FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Net income over the right share count, no coupons (derived) 0.837 0.553 0.378 0.373 0.353
Hybrid coupons per share -0.039 -0.040 -0.040 -0.034 -0.033
The second set's cross-check (company definition) 0.798 0.513 0.339 0.340 0.320
Effect of the model's inflated and phased share count -0.014 -0.013 -0.022 -0.022 -0.021
Model as it stands (first row plus this effect) 0.82 0.54 0.36 0.35 0.33

The same wrong entry does two further things. It makes the model pay the dividend per share on about 2,916m shares (about USD 1,590m a year against the USD 1,500m and USD 1,400m intended). And it adds USD 0.88bn of cash to the model's net debt in Q4 2026 that does not exist: the hybrid was issued in April 2026, its cash is already in FactSet's reported balances at 30 June and 30 September, and FactSet counts the hybrids as debt in any case (FactSet net debt USD 4,958m at Q3 2026 against the company's own USD 3,110m).

My three input changes, and why each.

  • Buybacks less new shares issued for cash, FY2026: from 889 to 7. Only the employee share issue is ordinary equity. This removes the 171m phantom shares, the overpaid dividends and the phantom cash.
  • Change in the share count other than through buybacks: 248.4 in FY2026, 0.0 in FY2027 (the second set had it in FY2027). Completion is expected around year-end 2026, and the second set's own text says FY2027 EPS "should use 2,744.8m for the whole year"; with the entry in FY2027 the model uses, on average, about half the new shares in FY2027 and overstates that year's EPS by about 4%. The cost of the move: if the model applies the shares in Q4 2026, its FY2026 EPS reads about USD 0.02 below the company's (about 0.78 against 0.80), and it may overstate the Q4 2026 dividend payment by about USD 40m. FY2026 is three-quarters reported and is not the year the valuation rests on, so I accept that.
  • Tax rate: 76.5, 80.5, 80.8, 80.0, 80.0 (from 75.4, 79.0, 78.5, 78.0, 78.0). The underlying tax view is unchanged. The extra 1.1 to 2.3 points stand in for the hybrid coupons, which this model has no line for: each year's addition is that year's coupon divided by pre-tax profit, so that the model's net income becomes the earnings attributable to ordinary shareholders and its EPS matches the company's definition (0.514, 0.338, 0.339, 0.321 for FY2027-FY2030; derived). I chose the tax line because it leaves revenue, EBITDA, EBITA and pre-tax profit untouched and because it also takes the coupons, which are paid in cash ahead of the ordinary dividend, out of the model's free cash flow. Two consequences the memo must carry. First, the tax-rate line in the model is no longer the tax rate: the tax view remains 79.0% for FY2027 and 78.0-78.5% after. Second, the model's "net income against consensus" will read about -10.5% in FY2027 and about -10% in FY2028; of that, 7 and 11 points are the coupons, which consensus net income does not deduct, and the like-for-like differences remain -3.6% and +0.9%.

The Financial Analyst should give the model a line for hybrid coupons (about 97, 110, 110, 92 and 90 for FY2026 to FY2030), deducted after net income and before EPS and paid in cash; when that line exists, the tax-rate input should revert to 75.4, 79.0, 78.5, 78.0 and 78.0.

The Financial Analyst should confirm the two model behaviours my share-count changes rest on, which I have inferred from the model's EPS and not seen: that "Buybacks less new shares issued for cash" creates ordinary shares at the current price, and that "Change in the share count" is phased evenly through the forecast quarters of the year it is entered in.

Free cash flow: every gap is the sum of two things the model cannot represent.

FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Cross-check, cash basis 3,615 1,870 665 1,400 1,335
Model as it stands 1,917 2,129 1,549 1,517 1,500
Gap (cross-check less model) +1,698 -259 -884 -117 -165
of which: cash tax paid differs from the tax charge (4,430, 6,165, 4,870, 3,945, 3,790 paid against charges of about 6,405, 5,711, 3,792, 3,633, 3,433) +1,975 -454 -1,078 -312 -357
of which: non-cash items inside net financial items that the model treats as cash (accretion on abandonment obligations; in FY2026 the reverse, booked currency gains) -97 +190 +190 +190 +190
of which: other operating items -180 +5 +4 +5 +2
  • The tax-payment lag is the whole story. Norwegian petroleum tax is paid in ten instalments, five in August to December of the income year and five in February to June of the next (two in Q1, three in Q2, two in Q3, three in Q4), which is why Var paid USD 1,140m in Q3 2026 and guides about USD 1,700m for Q4. In a year of rising income the cash paid lags the charge (FY2026: USD 2.0bn); in the following years it runs ahead. The model sets them equal, so it understates FY2026 free cash flow by USD 1.7bn and overstates FY2027-FY2030 by USD 1.4bn in total, of which USD 0.9bn in FY2028.
  • No forecast input is wrong, so I change none for this. The cash tax path is not an input line, and forcing it through the working-capital line would need working capital of 18-25% of revenue by FY2028-FY2030, which would mislead more than it would correct.
  • The second set's cash-tax path is confirmed by evidence it did not have. The tax payable was USD 3,313m at 30 September 2026, all of it on 2026 income (the 2025 liability was settled by June). Less the guided Q4 payment of 1,700 and plus Q4's current tax of about USD 1.8-2.2bn (Q3's was 1,749 on lower revenue but lower capital deductions), the amount left to pay in H1 2027 is about USD 3.4-3.8bn (derived); the company's own sensitivity table in the Q3 presentation gives USD 3,370m (Q1 1,350, Q2 2,020) at a USD 95 price and USD 4,490m (Q1 1,800, Q2 2,690) at USD 105. The second set used USD 3.5bn (3.2-3.8bn). I lean to about USD 3.6bn, phased two-fifths in Q1 and three-fifths in Q2, not evenly as the second set had it. The payable is in kroner and unhedged: a 5% move in USD/NOK moves the dollar amount by about USD 0.17bn.
  • How to read the model after the update (derived). From Q4 2026 to FY2030 the model's free cash flow will total about USD 0.7bn more than the cash basis after coupons. Its net debt will be about USD 0.75bn too high at end-FY2026 and USD 0.5bn too high at end-FY2027, then about USD 0.4bn too low at end-FY2028 and USD 0.7bn too low at end-FY2030. For leverage (about 0.5 times EBITDA) that is immaterial. For dividend cover it is decisive: the model will show FY2028 free cash flow of about 1,440 against dividends of 1,400, when the cash basis is 555 after coupons. The memo should take dividend cover and net debt from the cash basis in the table above, not from the model.

The Financial Analyst should carry cash tax separately from the tax charge: cash tax paid of 4,430 in FY2026 (Q4 2026: 1,700), 6,165 in FY2027 (about 1,440, 2,160, 1,025 and 1,540 by quarter), 4,870 in FY2028 (Q1 2028 about 1,500), 3,945 in FY2029 and 3,790 in FY2030; and should treat about USD 190m a year of the net financial items line from FY2027 as non-cash accretion.

The return at the guideline fair multiple after my changes (my estimate; the model update gives the figure of record). Two-year-forward EPS falls by about 4-6% (0.388 to about 0.366 today; 0.346 to about 0.334 at the 18-month column), so today's two-year-forward P/E rises from 13.4x to about 14.2x, and the total shareholder return at 9.41x falls from -24.3% to about -26% at the 18-month column and from -18.4% to about -20% at the three-year column (derived).

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Var Energi ASA (VAR-NO): Norwegian Continental Shelf oil and gas producer, majority-owned by Eni, reporting one upstream segment (about two-thirds liquids and one-third gas from the Balder area, North Sea, Norwegian Sea and Barents Sea hubs), combining with BlueNord ASA (a non-operated 36.8% interest in the Danish Underground Consortium, operated by TotalEnergies) around year-end 2026

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Var Energi ASA (VAR-NO), which is combining with BlueNord ASA around year-end 2026, set out broker by broker (Morgan Stanley, Goldman Sachs, Barclays, RBC Capital Markets, Nordea, DNB Carnegie, Norne Securities, Fearnley Securities, Clarksons Securities, Pareto Securities, ING, Jefferies and any other covering house) the latest published forecasts for 2027 and 2028 and how each is built: the Brent and TTF (or other European gas) price assumptions for 2027 and 2028 and the date they were set; the production assumed and whether BlueNord's volumes are included; the share count used for earnings per share (2,496.4 million before the combination or 2,744.8 million after it, per the merger announcement of 21 July 2026); whether the coupons on Var Energi's EUR 1.5 billion of hybrid securities are deducted before earnings per share; the depreciation, net financial items, tax rate and cash tax assumed; and the dividend per quarter assumed for 2027 and 2028. How do the brokers' price assumptions compare with the Brent and TTF forward curves in mid-November 2026 (Deutsche Bank reported Brent near USD 110 on 20 October 2026 and at USD 91.49 on 11 November 2026), and which brokers have changed their price assumptions, estimates, ratings or target prices for Var Energi since 21 October 2026, in which direction and for what stated reason? State clearly which brokers' research was and was not available.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Var Energi ASA (VAR-NO) and for European oil and gas producers generally (Equinor, Aker BP, Harbour Energy, Ithaca Energy, DNO, OKEA, Lundin Energy, BlueNord, Serica Energy, EnQuest), what happened to share prices, valuation multiples (forward price-to-earnings, EV/EBITDA, dividend yield, price to net asset value) and dividends in the 6, 12, 18 and 24 months after commodity price peaks: the European gas and oil price peak of 2022, the oil price peaks of 2014 and 2018, and any other relevant episode? For each company and episode: the multiple and dividend yield at the commodity peak and at the subsequent share-price trough; how long the trough took to arrive; whether the share price led or lagged the fall in consensus earnings estimates; whether the dividend was held, cut or raised, and at what commodity price and leverage; and what distinguished the companies whose shares held up from those that fell most. For Var Energi itself since its February 2022 listing: the range of its forward price-to-earnings multiple and dividend yield, when the highs and lows occurred, and what commodity prices, consensus revisions and broker ratings were at those points. What do brokers currently use as a through-cycle or fair valuation multiple, dividend yield or cost of equity for Var Energi, and what reasons do they give?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Var Energi ASA (VAR-NO), what do investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, currently say and do about the stock? Cover: the shareholder register outside Eni (which holds 63.04%, falling to 57.33% on completion of the BlueNord combination, per the announcement of 21 July 2026) and how the largest institutional holders' positions changed during 2026; short interest and stock-lending data and their trend; index membership and any index weight changes expected from the rise in free float to about 43% and from the 248.4 million new shares issued to BlueNord shareholders, and whether BlueNord's former holders are likely to keep or sell their Var Energi shares; any lock-up or stated holding commitment by Eni after completion, and how BP's sales of Aker BP shares in September and November 2026 (the 5 November 2026 placing was priced at NOK 311, a 12% discount to the prior close) were received by investors; and commentary on Var Energi from fund managers, investor letters, conference and roadshow feedback notes, financial media, and social media and retail investor forums (including Norwegian-language sources) during September to November 2026. What reasons do holders give for owning the stock, what are the main criticisms, and which topics (dividend cover in 2027 and 2028, the timing of Norwegian tax payments, the share count after the combination, hybrid capital, production guidance cuts, the Eni shareholding) are and are not discussed? State what evidence was available and what was not.
